</div></div><div class="sqs-block horizontalrule-block sqs-block-horizontalrule" data-block-type="47" id="block-aa00c68173769aed6e65"><div class="sqs-block-content"><hr/></div></div></div></div><div class="row sqs-row"><div class="col sqs-col-4 span-4"><div class="sqs-block html-block sqs-block-html" data-block-type="2" id="block-22df52e67ff4106956c9"><div class="sqs-block-content"><h2 style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Welcome</h2><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Welcome to Business Analyst Learnings!</p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">This is where you can get real, practical and timeless business analysis advice. Whether you are a budding BA or a seasoned professional, hang around and let's rub minds. We blog about all things business analysis. There will always be something for you to take away and possibly leave behind...</p></div></div><div class="sqs-block button-block sqs-block-button" data-block-type="53" id="block-533f64ac9c4f16020d00"><div class="sqs-block-content" id="yui_3_17_2_1_1571810019365_107"><div class="sqs-block-button-container--left" data-alignment="left" data-animation-tier="2" data-button-size="medium" id="yui_3_17_2_1_1571810019365_106">

</div></div></div></div><div class="col sqs-col-4 span-4"><div class="sqs-block html-block sqs-block-html" data-block-type="2" id="block-2788527858aa71ba9e38"><div class="sqs-block-content"><h2 style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Resources</h2><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Business Analysts need access to quality resources for their career development.</p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">We have assembled a host of resources you can download.</p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Visit the resources section for helpful downloads, links and useful templates. There is no need to start from scratch.</p></div></div><div class="sqs-block button-block sqs-block-button" data-block-type="53" id="block-1e09c16da79c2b1550ae"><div class="sqs-block-content" id="yui_3_17_2_1_1571810019365_115"><div class="sqs-block-button-container--left" data-alignment="left" data-animation-tier="2" data-button-size="medium" id="yui_3_17_2_1_1571810019365_114">
<a class="sqs-block-button-element--medium sqs-block-button-element" data-initialized="true" href="/freeresources">Resources</a>
</div></div></div><div class="sqs-block horizontalrule-block sqs-block-horizontalrule" data-block-type="47" id="block-yui_3_17_2_2_1420402923199_29398"><div class="sqs-block-content"><hr/></div></div></div><div class="col sqs-col-4 span-4"><div class="sqs-block html-block sqs-block-html" data-block-type="2" id="block-07aedbe8952ee61a28be"><div class="sqs-block-content"><h2 style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Contact</h2><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Businesses are faced with diverse challenges and an increasing pressure to deliver value under tight deadlines and with limited resources. Business Analysis is crucial to understanding impediments to process efficiency and proffering effective solutions.</p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Have a question? <!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2019/10/20/the-language-of-requirements-documents-when-to-use-shall-will-amp-should">The Language Of Requirements Documents - When To Use Shall, Will &amp; Should.</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">A well-written <a href="https://qracorp.com/write-clear-requirements-document/">business requirements document (BRD)</a> forms the basis of successful project documents and can be used to verify that all stakeholders are on the same page. Three words that are often misunderstood and therefore misused in requirement documents are <em>shall</em>, <em>should</em> and <em>will</em>. </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">But first… <strong>Guest Post</strong></p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2019/7/19/waterfall-to-agile-a-necessary-mindset-shift-for-business-analysts">Waterfall to Agile: A Necessary Mindset Shift For Business Analysts</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Moving from the traditional/waterfall approach to software development to an agile approach certainly involves a mindset shift that may not always come naturally or even easily for Business Analysts. This mental shift comes with implicit obligations of adopting new ways of working.</p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2019/4/25/how-do-business-analysts-fit-into-agile">How Do Business Analysts Fit Into Agile Projects?</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Though the Agile Methodology is used heavily in software development domains, it has since spread into different sectors. With Agile comes a growing emphasis on equipping the development team, e.g. your software developers, content writers, engineers, designers, etc. with the information, tools, and direction they need to produce a tangible output within a fixed period of time, that is, sprints of typically 2 weeks. <strong>Guest Post By Greg Kellner</strong></p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2019/2/23/4-basic-tips-for-writing-a-better-business-case">4 Basic Tips For Writing A Better Business Case </a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Business Analysts are often required to write business cases to justify whether or not a concept/product is viable. Business cases can be of varying lengths and structure. Taking on such a task can seem intimidating, but it doesn’t have to be. Here are four basic but easily forgotten tips that will make the process easier, and result in a winning business case.</p>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2019/1/12/how-to-implement-a-successful-business-intelligence-strategy">8 Tips For Implementing A Successful Business Intelligence Strategy</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p style="white-space: pre-wrap;">In order to keep your business competitive, drive growth, ensure continuous improvement and enhance profits, a Business Intelligence (BI) strategy is essential. But a BI strategy is not just about technology or choosing the right platform. Like any part of your business, BI requires strategy, planning, buy-in, execution and continuous review. In this blog post, we will look at the key steps to implementing a business intelligence strategy.</p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2018/5/6/5-simple-ways-bas-can-avoid-repeating-mistakes-from-past-projects">5 Simple Ways BAs Can Avoid Repeating Mistakes From Past Projects</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Once you embark on a new business analyst job or project, chances are that you will try to avoid past mistakes and look for ways in which you can deliver better results. </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">A lot can be said for this motivation. The beginning of a project is usually an opportunity for a fresh start. Regardless of how advanced your business analysis skills are, or how mature your processes are, there is always room to do better. <strong>Guest post by Jason Hartman.</strong></p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/blog/2018/5/6/5-simple-ways-bas-can-avoid-repeating-mistakes-from-past-projects">Read More →</a>

<div class="summary-thumbnail-outer-container">

<div class="summary-thumbnail img-wrapper" style="overflow: hidden; padding-bottom: 100%;">
<!-- Main Image -->
<img alt="How to Get Stakeholders to Open Up During Elicitation Sessions" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5add70828a922d433d09dabd/1566477959634/" data-image-dimensions="2500x1668" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5add70828a922d433d09dabd/1566477959634/" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5add70828a922d433d09dabd/1566477959634/?format=300w" style="left: -44.3933px; top: 0px; width: 266.787px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2018/4/23/how-to-get-stakeholders-to-open-up-during-elicitation-sessions">How to Get Stakeholders to Open Up During Elicitation Sessions</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>If you’ve worked as a Business Analyst for a while, you’ve probably encountered stakeholders who appear quite economical with information. You know there’s more to the issue than they’re letting on. The key question is, how can you get stakeholders to open up when conducting elicitation events like interviews or workshop sessions? </p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/blog/2018/4/23/how-to-get-stakeholders-to-open-up-during-elicitation-sessions">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2018/4/17/what-business-analysts-can-learn-from-technical-writers">What Business Analysts Can Learn From Technical Writers</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Most of my time, especially at the tail end of software development projects, is spent preparing user guides for end users. I try to be creative by including screenshots and business process models so that readers can completely understand what the automated processes are about, and how the supporting business applications work. As a BA, you may find yourself in a situation where you need to prepare user guides for end users. </p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2018/3/31/the-business-analysts-transferrable-skills">The Business Analyst’s Transferrable Skills</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Transferrable skills are a core set of skills and abilities, which can be applied to other jobs and industries. Though most examples you will see are related to soft skills like communication, listening, leadership, etc, transferrable skills extend beyond these and it’s extremely important to identify them when thinking of a career change or advancement.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/blog/2018/3/31/the-business-analysts-transferrable-skills">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2018/1/10/what-business-analysts-can-learn-from-the-swiss-cheese-model">What Business Analysts Can Learn From Swiss Cheese</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Failures can come from any aspect of an organization and they may be interconnected. A failed software implementation may have happened for multiple reasons. For example, bad project management, inaccurate requirements, poor training, etc</p>
</div>

<a class="summary-thumbnail-container sqs-gallery-image-container" data-description="" data-title="How Business Analysts Can Avoid Confirmation Bias" href="/blog/how-business-analysts-can-avoid-confirmation-bias">
<div class="summary-thumbnail img-wrapper" style="overflow: hidden; padding-bottom: 100%;">
<!-- Main Image -->
<img alt="How Business Analysts Can Avoid Confirmation Bias" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5a4a8b7341920273aba009b5/1566476894063/Depositphotos_123983780_m-2015.jpg" data-image-dimensions="1000x667" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5a4a8b7341920273aba009b5/1566476894063/Depositphotos_123983780_m-2015.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5a4a8b7341920273aba009b5/1566476894063/Depositphotos_123983780_m-2015.jpg?format=300w" style="left: -44.4333px; top: 0px; width: 266.867px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/how-business-analysts-can-avoid-confirmation-bias">How Business Analysts Can Avoid Confirmation Bias</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>When we have a strong belief about something or a bias towards a particular opinion, we consciously or unconsciously seek out evidence that validates what we already believe. When we come across contrary evidence, our default behaviour is to ignore it, diminish it or in some cases, conclude that it’s wrong prematurely without exploring its merits.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/blog/how-business-analysts-can-avoid-confirmation-bias">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/12/30/eliciting-requirements-from-dispersed-stakeholders">Eliciting Requirements From Dispersed Stakeholders</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">

<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">

<div class="summary-excerpt">

<!-- Main Image -->
<img alt="Business Analysts &amp; The Anchor Effect" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5a34b8c89140b76437c264aa/1514628708234/Depositphotos_7002796_m-2015.jpg" data-image-dimensions="1000x667" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5a34b8c89140b76437c264aa/1514628708234/Depositphotos_7002796_m-2015.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/5a34b8c89140b76437c264aa/1514628708234/Depositphotos_7002796_m-2015.jpg?format=300w" style="left: -44.4333px; top: 0px; width: 266.867px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/12/16/business-analysts-the-anchor-effect">Business Analysts &amp; The Anchor Effect</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Imagine a component in your car requires a fix. You approach your mechanic and he gives you a quote. This quote (assuming you’ve never had to pay for this part before and you have no prior knowledge of how much it costs) becomes the reference point from which you begin negotiation. This reference point is called an anchor and its effects can be seen in everyday situations.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/blog/2017/12/16/business-analysts-the-anchor-effect">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/12/4/what-bas-should-know-about-feedback-analysis">What BAs Should Know About Feedback Analysis</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>I particularly find the concept of feedback analysis quite versatile because it can be applied to one’s professional development as well as the concept of implementing business projects successfully over time. <em>Feedback analysis can give insight into your performance, whether as an individual or as a team.</em></p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/9/11/a-business-analysts-guide-to-managing-change-requests">A Business Analyst's Guide To Managing Change Requests</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>It’s common knowledge that people tend to resist change as much as they can. However, without change, there can’t be progress and BAs would certainly not have that much to do. When working on large projects, change requests from stakeholders are to be expected. Successful project managers and analysts know how to manage them without bringing the project to a standstill.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/blog/2017/9/11/a-business-analysts-guide-to-managing-change-requests">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/8/17/business-architecture-whats-in-it-for-business-analysts">Business Architecture: What's In It For Business Analysts?</a></div>

<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>How can BAs ensure requirements are practical, achievable and in line with the strategy and capabilities of the business?</p><p>The Business Architecture offers a starting point.</p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/8/15/a-list-of-business-process-management-certifications">A List of Business Process Management Certifications</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>While there’s nothing like having some experience under your belt, Business Process Management (BPM) certifications can aid analysts in some key ways: they serve as concrete evidence of business process management knowledge; offer the opportunity to learn new concepts /best practices; and benefit from the experience of trainers/mentors.</p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/7/4/requirements-risk-management">Requirements Risk Management: What's In It For Business Analysts?</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>When elicitation focuses on identifying exceptions and failure points, it’s a form of risk management. The idea is to find potential solutions to these questions and include them as part of requirements to reduce the probability of missing out on critical requirements.</p>

<img alt="The Business Analyst &amp; Project Manager Collaboration: Identifying The Various Costs Involved In A Business Case" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/590dc64917bffcc3aa0e72c1/1514625232523/Depositphotos_138527976_m-2015.jpg" data-image-dimensions="1000x756" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/590dc64917bffcc3aa0e72c1/1514625232523/Depositphotos_138527976_m-2015.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/590dc64917bffcc3aa0e72c1/1514625232523/Depositphotos_138527976_m-2015.jpg?format=300w" style="left: -28.7249px; top: 0px; width: 235.45px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/5/6/the-business-analystproject-manager-collaboration-identifying-the-various-costs-involved-in-writing-a-business-case">The Business Analyst &amp; Project Manager Collaboration: Identifying The Various Costs Involved In A Business Case</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>In preparing a business case, the business analyst is often required to conduct some measure of cost analysis. When highlighting the benefits associated with an initiative, it is extremely important to also indicate the costs that will go along with implementing the initiative. Information regarding costs is particularly important in making go or no-go decisions when deciding which projects to implement. The business analyst should work with the project manager to identify these costs and arrive at realistic estimates.</p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/4/21/the-role-of-a-ba-in-managing-stakeholder-expectations">The Role Of A BA In Managing Stakeholder Expectations</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Managing stakeholder expectations is essential to business projects' success. If you know ahead what stakeholders “expect” the system to do, you can advise them of this well ahead of time to avoid disappointments further down the road.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/blog/2017/4/21/the-role-of-a-ba-in-managing-stakeholder-expectations">Read More →</a>

<div class="summary-item summary-item-record-type-text sqs-gallery-design-carousel-slide summary-item-has-thumbnail summary-item-has-excerpt summary-item-has-cats summary-item-has-tags summary-item-has-author summary-item-has-comments-enabled" data-type="image" id="yui_3_17_2_1_1571810019365_238" style="width: 25.25%; padding-left: 1%; padding-right: 1%;">
<!-- Thumbnail -->
<div class="summary-thumbnail-outer-container">
<a class="summary-thumbnail-container sqs-gallery-image-container" data-description="" data-title="How Should BAs Relate With Other Project Stakeholders?" href="/blog/2017/4/11/how-should-bas-relate-with-other-project-stakeholders">
<div class="summary-thumbnail img-wrapper" style="overflow: hidden; padding-bottom: 100%;">
<!-- Main Image -->
<img alt="How Should BAs Relate With Other Project Stakeholders?" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/58ec857a2e69cffbfbeb0184/1514619298488/ID-100275905.jpg" data-image-dimensions="400x266" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/58ec857a2e69cffbfbeb0184/1514619298488/ID-100275905.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/50c9c511e4b0a97682fac90c/58ec857a2e69cffbfbeb0184/1514619298488/ID-100275905.jpg?format=300w" style="left: -44.8346px; top: 0px; width: 267.669px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>
</div>
</a>
<!-- Products: Quick View -->
</div>
<div class="summary-content sqs-gallery-meta-container">
<!-- Metadata (Above Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--above-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/blog/2017/4/11/how-should-bas-relate-with-other-project-stakeholders">How Should BAs Relate With Other Project Stakeholders?</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>According to Stephen Ward &amp; Chris Chapman in their piece, <a href="http://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/01446190801998708">Stakeholders and uncertainty management in projects</a>, “Stakeholders are a major source of uncertainty on projects and this uncertainty stems from whom these stakeholders are, their motives, and how they can influence a project.”</p><p>It is this uncertainty that constitutes risks to the project. BAs should relate with other project stakeholders to overcome this uncertainty by improving their ability to convince stakeholders to see things their way, thereby influencing the course of the project.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/blog/2017/4/11/how-should-bas-relate-with-other-project-stakeholders">Read More →</a>

<a class="summary-thumbnail-container sqs-gallery-image-container" data-description="" data-title="Do Law Graduates Make Good Business Analysts?" href="/business-analyst-career/2019/6/12/do-law-graduates-make-good-business-analysts">

<!-- Main Image -->
<img alt="Do Law Graduates Make Good Business Analysts?" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/52dc15e0e4b0826240aa0d74/5d00df6226b4cb000136600a/1566909639333/" data-image-dimensions="2500x1667" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/52dc15e0e4b0826240aa0d74/5d00df6226b4cb000136600a/1566909639333/" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/52dc15e0e4b0826240aa0d74/5d00df6226b4cb000136600a/1566909639333/?format=300w" style="left: -44.4733px; top: 0px; width: 266.947px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2019/6/12/do-law-graduates-make-good-business-analysts">Do Law Graduates Make Good Business Analysts?</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Business Analyst roles across different organizations can be quite different depending on the organization and industry sector. A good business analyst needs a wide variety of skills across multiple domains to be effective and it really is possible to<a href="http://www.businessanalystbootcamp.com/how-do-i-transition-from-law-degree-to-business-analyst-in-uk/"> <span style="text-decoration:underline">be a law graduate and make a transition</span></a> from legal professional to business analyst.</p>
</div>

<div class="summary-thumbnail img-wrapper" style="overflow: hidden; padding-bottom: 100%;">
<!-- Main Image -->
<img alt="What Data Analysts Can Do With a Psych Degree" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/52dc15e0e4b0826240aa0d74/5cdaafda0d9297dd1ba911ea/1566909853310/" data-image-dimensions="2500x1667" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/52dc15e0e4b0826240aa0d74/5cdaafda0d9297dd1ba911ea/1566909853310/" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/52dc15e0e4b0826240aa0d74/5cdaafda0d9297dd1ba911ea/1566909853310/?format=300w" style="left: -44.4733px; top: 0px; width: 266.947px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2019/5/14/what-data-analysts-can-do-with-a-psych-degree">What Data Analysts Can Do With a Psych Degree</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Data analysis seems like it should be all about the hard numbers. That’s what businesses are typically after: accurate, immutable facts that assist in guiding decision-making. Unfortunately, as is the case with anything involving the human race, business data is easily influenced in one way or another by those people producing the data as well as those people analyzing it. That’s why it’s important for data analysts in any field to understand psychology.</p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2019/5/3/key-fundamentals-to-successfully-pursuing-a-career-as-a-business-analyst">Key Fundamentals To Successfully Pursuing A Career As A Business Analyst</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">If you landed on this page because you Googled something along the lines of “<em>What it takes to become a business analyst</em>,” then you've arrived at the site with all the information you need to know on what it's all about and how to become one. </p><p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">This article focuses on giving a few crucial pointers to those who would like to pursue a career as a business analyst. </p>
</div>

<div class="summary-thumbnail-outer-container">
<a class="summary-thumbnail-container sqs-gallery-image-container" data-description="" data-title="Develop Your Writing Skills: Mistakes To Avoid" href="/business-analyst-career/2019/4/28/develop-your-writing-skills-mistakes-to-avoid">

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2019/4/28/develop-your-writing-skills-mistakes-to-avoid">Develop Your Writing Skills: Mistakes To Avoid</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Communicating effectively with stakeholders and BA teams is one of the most essential skills a business analyst can possess. Written communication, in particular, is of vital importance, as there can be no room for error. Any details that must be communicated clearly are best written down, so as to avoid any confusion. </p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2018/12/31/how-lean-amp-six-sigma-can-boost-your-business-analysis-career">How Lean &amp; Six Sigma Can Boost Your Business Analysis Career</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Lean &amp; Six Sigma methods are preferred by most organizations for process improvement efforts and ensuring the successful completion of projects. Applied initially to manufacturing industry processes, Lean &amp; Six Sigma can now be used in almost every domain of an organization for continuous process improvement.</p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2018/1/15/how-do-mistakes-happen">How do mistakes happen?</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">

</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>We all make mistakes. In fact, that’s how we learn. It’s however extremely important to understand what mistakes are, the different types of mistakes and how they happen so that we can learn from them and prevent them from happening again in the future.</p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2018/1/15/an-introverts-guide-to-participating-in-business-meetings">An Introvert’s Guide To Participating In Business Meetings</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Introverts are a special group of people who need sufficient time to make sense of a situation, reflect and analyze information. They need to process information before taking a position on issues. Business meetings can seem chaotic to them with different people going off in different directions and throwing up suggestions that don’t seem well thought out.</p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2018/1/13/saydo-ratio-how-to-be-more-reliable">Say:Do Ratio - How To Be More Reliable</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>The Say:Do ratio is a concept that seeks to rate a person’s reliability by comparing what they say they’ll do to what they actually do: The ‘Say:Do' ratio. It’s all about keeping your promises and doing what you say you’ll do.</p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2017/10/23/what-bas-should-know-about-managing-business-meetings">What BAs Should Know About Managing Business Meetings</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>One of the core competencies business analysts should have is the ability to facilitate business meetings. From elicitation to analysis, meetings are a true test of a business analyst’s facilitation skills. As a business analyst, you may love meetings, or you may hate them, but they aren’t going anywhere anytime soon, so it’s best to learn how to organize and run meetings effectively.</p>

<div class="summary-title">

<p>Listening is one of the most powerful skills that any business analyst can have in his or her toolbox, and the technique of active listening has been helping business professionals become better listeners since Carl Rogers and Richard Farson coined the term “active listening” in 1987.  </p>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2017/9/30/6-resume-writing-tips-business-analysts-should-not-take-for-granted">6 Resume Writing Tips Business Analysts Should Not Take For Granted </a></div>

<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>An average US company spends <a href="https://www.glassdoor.com/employers/popular-topics/hr-stats.htm">$4,000</a> to fill an open position. Here are 6 resume writing secrets that can help you land an interview and get a job as a business analysts.</p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2017/9/21/7-key-ways-a-business-process-analyst-can-improve-your-companys-output">7 Key Ways A Business Process Analyst Can Improve Your Company’s Output</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>To remain significant as a business, you need to make existing processes efficient and lean. Of course, processes are inherent in every organization, but they can be cumbersome and not achieve their set goals if not executed the right way or you don't have the required resources to spearhead process change efforts. Guest Post By <strong>Owen McGab Enaohwo</strong></p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2017/9/16/tips-for-business-analyst-job-hunting">Tips For Business Analyst Job Hunting</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>There are few things in life more daunting than hunting for a new job. Heart palpitations while in front of a panel of interviewers and trying to present yourself in the best possible light is difficult enough as it is, even without knowing that you’re just one of dozens—if not hundreds—of candidates.  </p>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>One possible reason workplace conflicts are so common is the inability of people who are responsible for handling them to deal with conflicts as well as they should. What are some of the most important conflict-resolution strategies and techniques that managers and non-managers alike can use to avoid conflicts at the workplace? Here's a list that every BA can learn from.</p>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/business-analyst-career/2017/6/6/top-skills-business-analysts-should-master">Top Skills Business Analysts Should Master </a></div>

<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>The U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics <a href="http://www.iiba.org/Careers/Becoming-a-Business-Analyst.aspx">estimates</a> that American employers will need about 876,000 business analysis-related professionals by 2020. The demand for business analysts is clearly here, but the question is, who will take the best jobs?</p>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2019/1/12/how-to-determine-if-your-company-really-needs-a-new-erp-system">How To Determine If Your Company Really Needs A New ERP System</a></div>

<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">What is an enterprise resource planning (ERP) system, and how do you determine if your company <em>really </em>needs one? First, let’s define what an ERP system is. Then we can look into why you would need one and whether the benefits of an ERP system for your company would more than justify the time and expense of selecting and implementing one.  </p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2018/12/31/big-data-analytical-software-vs-statisticians">Big Data: Analytical Software vs. Statisticians</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">There are a few choices available to businesses in the world of analytics: you can use powerful, modern predictive analytics software, you can employ an actual statistician, or you can use a blended approach with both software and statisticians. There are pros and cons to each method, and the one that is right for your business will not only depend on what type of business you have but also how you use data. <strong>Guest Post By Devin Morrissey</strong></p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2018/11/24/what-is-blockchain-technology">What Is Blockchain Technology?</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">

<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p style="white-space: pre-wrap;">With the way <em>blockchain</em> is thrown around as a buzzword in most technology headlines, an uninformed party might presume that it's an all-knowing artificial intelligence machine that can do anything from powering Bitcoin to tying your shoe laces. The technology can also confuse anyone who isn’t fluent in advanced IT lingo. Blockchain is the technology behind Bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies, but what does it mean? <strong>Sponsored Post</strong></p>

<img alt="So, What's An API?" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/51dc6f4ae4b0a512f81e72c3/5992cd31be42d61afe0f249a/1514702909113/Depositphotos_73335585_m-2015.jpg" data-image-dimensions="1000x883" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/51dc6f4ae4b0a512f81e72c3/5992cd31be42d61afe0f249a/1514702909113/Depositphotos_73335585_m-2015.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/51dc6f4ae4b0a512f81e72c3/5992cd31be42d61afe0f249a/1514702909113/Depositphotos_73335585_m-2015.jpg?format=300w" style="left: -11.7928px; top: 0px; width: 201.586px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2017/8/15/so-whats-an-api">So, What's An API?</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>In some cases, a software application will need to send data to or receive data from another. How does one achieve this integration between two separate applications built with separate development languages? An application programming interface (API) is a set of routines for achieving this. It specifies how software components should communicate with one another.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/technology-matters/2017/8/15/so-whats-an-api">Read More →</a>

<div class="summary-excerpt">

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2017/7/4/a-list-of-free-requirements-management-rm-software">A List Of Free Requirements Management (RM) Software</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>There are plenty of try-before-you-pay-a-lot offers for Requirement Management Software. Good, free RM software, however, is rare. This article sketches out three of the most promising packages and informs you of the pros and cons of each. Not all of them are winners. But there are still some excellent, free bargains available. Let’s see what’s out there.</p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2017/5/1/a-business-analysts-review-of-lucidchart">A Business Analyst’s Review of Lucidchart</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Communicating requirements visually is a very effective way of communicating with stakeholders. However, if you’re not a designer, creating beautiful visual diagrams, charts, plans, and maps can seem like a challenge. Luckily, we have tools like Lucidchart, that make it simple to communicate with stakeholders using easy-to-use tools and pre-designed templates.</p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2017/2/26/the-use-of-data-models-on-software-projects-why-bother">The Use Of Data Models On Software Projects: Why Bother?</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Since the invention and introduction of the entity-relationship modelling technique by Peter Chen in 1970s, data modelling techniques have been used to represent information requirements of an organization as well as describe how data should be used to meet the requirements given by the end user.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/technology-matters/2017/2/26/the-use-of-data-models-on-software-projects-why-bother">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2017/1/22/a-list-of-free-software-for-design-mockups">A List Of Free Software For Design Mockups</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>You shouldn’t have to create an excellent design from scratch. Most apps are the result of iterative design, a methodology based on a cyclic process of prototyping, testing, analyzing, and refining. Design mockups play a significant role in regards to this methodology, allowing designers to create example designs in a fraction of the time it would take using traditional tools.</p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/technology-matters/2016/12/8/the-difference-between-software-validation-and-verification">The Difference Between Software Validation and Verification</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Software requirements validation and verification are both complementary processes related to the concepts of quality assurance. Often abbreviated as V&amp;V, the combined process of validation and verification is used in software project management, engineering, and testing to ensure that all specifications are met and the product fulfils its purpose.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/technology-matters/2016/12/8/the-difference-between-software-validation-and-verification">Read More →</a>
<!-- Metadata (Below Content) -->

</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
</div> <!-- End .summary-content -->
</div> <!-- End .summary-item -->
</div> <!-- End .summary-item-list -->
</div> <!-- End .summary-item-list-container -->
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2019/7/19/demystifying-the-product-backlog">Demystifying The Product Backlog</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Within the context of agile software development, the product backlog is a platform where all the potential work (product backlog items) that need to be delivered are recorded, tracked and prioritized. Though owned by the Product Owner, anyone may suggest items to add.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/ba-techniques/2019/7/19/demystifying-the-product-backlog">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2018/10/7/failure-mode-and-effects-analysis-fmea-a-beginners-guide">Failure Mode and Effects Analysis (FMEA): A Beginner’s Guide</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Failure Mode and Effects Analysis (FMEA) is a proactive technique that can be applied to the early detection of failures or defects in products and services. It is a systematic risk assessment process used by analysts looking to reduce the chances of faults by detecting problems and their possible repercussions in time for remediation.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/ba-techniques/2018/10/7/failure-mode-and-effects-analysis-fmea-a-beginners-guide">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2018/1/12/roles-permissions-matrix">Roles &amp; Permissions Matrix</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->

</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/10/24/qa-quality-assurance-versus-qc-quality-control">QA (Quality assurance) Versus QC (Quality control) </a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>The terms “Quality Assurance (QA)" and “Quality Control (QC)” can be confusing or even misunderstood to mean the same thing. In this article, the goal is to clarify the real meaning of the two terms and explain their differences.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/10/24/qa-quality-assurance-versus-qc-quality-control">Read More →</a>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/10/2/network-analysis-demystified">Network Analysis Demystified </a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Network analysis is <a href="http://www.businessdictionary.com/definition/network-analysis.html">defined</a> as the process of “breaking down a complex project’s data into its parts (activities, events, durations, etc.) and plotting them to show their interdependencies and interrelationships.”</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->

</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Originally released in 2005 by BPM Group, the 8-Omega framework has been adapted and embraced by a large number of organizations around the world for the improvement of business processes.</p>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/9/23/requirements-scope-statements">Requirements Scope Statements</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Requirements scope statements are defined later in the project life cycle and are relatively fluid. As long as they still fall within the boundaries set by project scope statements, they can be updated and modified to reflect the changing needs of the project.</p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/9/18/an-introduction-to-scrs">An Introduction To SCRS</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>The SCRS approach encourages analysts to present practical and feasible business solutions that flow from the current strategy and are in tune with the business’ overarching vision and goals. Think of SCRS as an action plan for solving business problems.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/9/18/an-introduction-to-scrs">Read More →</a>
<!-- Metadata (Below Content) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-content">

<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">

<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Consequently, systems developed using the traditional approaches to software development are rigid and difficult to change. It’s often necessary to modify a large number of parts of the system just to implement a single, small change. The traditional approaches to software development are effective only in situations where requirements are specific and unlikely to change over time.</p>

<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Some processes, problems, and projects appear complicated, yet their complexity disappears as soon as one takes a closer look and breaks them down into simpler parts. This is the objective of functional decomposition - to clarify how the overall functionality emerges from the interaction between individual components in increasing detail.</p>

<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">

</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>The key importance of problem tracking is seldom evident when there are just two or three problems to overcome at the same time; it doesn’t take much to stay organized in such situations. It gets much more complicated, however, when the number of problems increases, especially in the early phases of software development projects.</p>

<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>In a perfect world, our plans would always go according to our expectations, and nobody would ever make a wrong decision. Sadly, we don’t live in a perfect world, and things don’t always go according to plan. </div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Six Sigma has continued evolving over the years, and focuses on improving customer satisfaction, meeting stakeholders' requirements, and improving the outputs (products/services) offered by organizations.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/6/5/an-introduction-to-six-sigma">Read More →</a>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/5/20/problemissue-tracking-technique">Problem/Issue Tracking Technique </a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>This technique is also known as “Item tracking” or “Issue Tracking" and it covers the supervision of defects, assumptions, actions and issues until they are resolved. It provides an opportunity for stakeholders to rank the importance of issues affecting them.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/5/20/problemissue-tracking-technique">Read More →</a>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/5/3/project-estimation-techniques-business-analysts-should-know-about-">Project Estimation Techniques Business Analysts Should Know About   </a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>In developing a business case or a feasibility study, many activities are associated with inherent uncertainties and therefore, estimating the resources required to complete such project activities can be a challenging task for the business analyst.</p><p>Great project estimation commonly involves applying one or more techniques in arriving at time and cost estimations, some of which are discussed in this article.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->

<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Business analysts can use VRIO to accurately assess the internal resources of a business, its competitive advantage potential, and the possibilities of improvement of relevant resources within key business areas.</p>

<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->

<p>Personas are particularly useful because they serve the purpose of constantly reminding the design team whom they are building the solution for, plus they are easier to remember since there’s a story behind each persona.</p>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/ba-techniques/2017/3/26/use-of-personas-in-software-development">Use Of Personas In Software Development</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Initially <a href="http://dev.cooper.com/journal/2016/10/the_origin_of_personas">introduced by Alan Cooper</a>, an American software designer and programmer who is widely known as the “Father of Visual Basic,” personas are examples of fictitious users who represent a subset of users.</p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/project-matters/2019/4/17/tips-for-acing-your-pmp-exam-how-i-passed">Tips For Acing Your PMP Exam - How I Passed</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p style="white-space:pre-wrap;">I finally passed my PMP exam today with an “Above Target” rating in all 5 process groups. Attaining the PMP certification is a journey I was on for quite a while, I must say. I set the goal a couple of years back but was too busy to do much about it. It feels fulfilling now that it’s done and dusted. I’ll outline in this post, what helped and what didn’t, especially for those on the path to getting certified. I hope this helps.</p>
</div>

<!-- Main Image -->
<img alt="5 Must-Know Tips For Project Management Newbies" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/54c36011e4b0f7f4ab5c16d8/5c694cae4785d32318b8a1e7/1567049370847/" data-image-dimensions="2500x1666" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.0" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/54c36011e4b0f7f4ab5c16d8/5c694cae4785d32318b8a1e7/1567049370847/" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/54c36011e4b0f7f4ab5c16d8/5c694cae4785d32318b8a1e7/1567049370847/?format=300w" style="left: -44.5534px; top: 0px; width: 267.107px; height: 178px; position: absolute;"/>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/project-matters/2019/2/17/5-must-know-tips-for-project-management-newbies">5 Must-Know Tips For Project Management Newbies</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">As a project manager, you will be expected to come up with project plans. While plans certainly have their downfalls—they are hypothetical, constrain future ideas, and are perpetually out-of-date—they certainly have their uses. In fact, your company or client will need to review the project plan in order to determine when tasks will be completed, how much the project may cost and validate the project deliverables/scope. <strong>Guest Post By Ben Aston.</strong></p>
</div>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/project-matters/2018/12/16/4-best-project-management-tools-for-smbs">4 Best Project Management Tools for SMBs</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p class="" style="white-space:pre-wrap;">Understanding exactly what your business needs to work more efficiently as you’re navigating projects is key to choosing tools that will support (and not hinder) your goals. Adding a software solution just to have more tools, without understanding exactly what you truly need may, in fact, cause increased or unnecessary costs and frustration to your small team. <strong>Guest Post By Jeni Rogers</strong></p>

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/project-matters/2017/6/11/why-projects-fail-the-role-of-project-managers">Why Projects Fail &amp; The Role of Project Managers</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Completing a project is never easy. Even a project you are working on by yourself can prove difficult to complete. How many times have you started a project and given up halfway through? Well, imagine how difficult it is for businesses with so many different people and departments working on projects—the high failure rates are hardly surprising. This infographic from <strong><a href="http://www.trainwest.com.au/">Trainwest</a> </strong>takes you through why projects fail and what project managers can do to keep project teams motivated.</p>
</div>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/agile-matters/2018/11/18/the-2018-guide-to-pursuing-a-full-agile-software-lifecycle">The 2018 Guide To Pursuing A Full Agile Software Lifecycle</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p style="white-space: pre-wrap;">The term, <em>Agile,</em> in software development refers to a group of development processes based on solving problems through collaboration across teams. It is a framework that encourages developers to work together, stay accountable, and constantly seek improvements. Using Agile is a way to compete in a world of constant demand. </p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/agile-matters/2018/11/18/the-2018-guide-to-pursuing-a-full-agile-software-lifecycle">Read More →</a>

<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/agile-matters/2018/11/4/incorporating-kanban-principles-in-software-development">Incorporating Kanban Principles In Software Development</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Kanban is an agile methodology that involves constant communication amongst team members to ensure obstacles are minimized, especially while projects are ongoing. It also involves matching the amount of work in progress to the team’s capacity. </p>

<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p style="white-space: pre-wrap;">Scrum is one of the most widely used frameworks aligned to Agile and is adopted extensively across software development teams. </p><p style="white-space: pre-wrap;">This article highlights some characteristics of scrum teams you should be aware of if you are a business analyst and you find yourself working as part of a scrum team:</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->

<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/agile-matters/2017/6/26/how-agile-lean-practices-affect-business-analysts">How Agile &amp; Lean Practices Affect Business Analysts</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>The role of business analysts can be aligned with agile practices in a way that benefits everyone involved. BAs who have been recently thrust in an agile environment should familiarize themselves with the principles and practices of agile, and go the extra mile to develop competencies that go beyond what is expected of them on traditional or waterfall-based projects.</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/agile-matters/2017/6/26/how-agile-lean-practices-affect-business-analysts">Read More →</a>

<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">

<!-- Thumbnail -->
<div class="summary-thumbnail-outer-container">
<a class="summary-thumbnail-container sqs-gallery-image-container" data-description="" data-title="Don't Let Governance Threaten Your Agile Transformation" href="/agile-matters/2017/5/29/dont-let-governance-threaten-your-agile-transformation">
<div class="summary-thumbnail img-wrapper" style="overflow: hidden; padding-bottom: 66.6667%;">
<!-- Main Image -->
<img alt="Don't Let Governance Threaten Your Agile Transformation" class="summary-thumbnail-image loaded" data-image="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/5bf0ad286d2a7391b5a103d2/592c4aa7d2b857159c941422/1514625871003/governance.jpg" data-image-dimensions="500x333" data-image-focal-point="0.5,0.5" data-image-resolution="300w" data-load="false" data-parent-ratio="1.5" data-src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/5bf0ad286d2a7391b5a103d2/592c4aa7d2b857159c941422/1514625871003/governance.jpg" src="https://static1.squarespace.com/static/50c9c50fe4b0a97682fac903/5bf0ad286d2a7391b5a103d2/592c4aa7d2b857159c941422/1514625871003/governance.jpg?format=300w" style="left: -0.339339px; top: 0px; width: 178.679px; height: 119px; position: absolute;"/>
</div>
</a>
<!-- Products: Quick View -->
</div>
<div class="summary-content sqs-gallery-meta-container">
<!-- Metadata (Above Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--above-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">

</div>
<!-- Title -->
<div class="summary-title">
<a class="summary-title-link" href="/agile-matters/2017/5/29/dont-let-governance-threaten-your-agile-transformation">Don't Let Governance Threaten Your Agile Transformation</a></div>
<!-- Metadata (Below Title) -->
<div class="summary-metadata-container summary-metadata-container--below-title">
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
</div>
<!-- Excerpt -->
<div class="summary-excerpt">
<p>Governance seems to be one of those frightening words that threaten to stop an Agile transformation effort dead in its tracks. I’ve been hearing it whispered, and even screamed once or twice, quite a lot recently. There’s no big surprise here. As big corporations and Government agencies get increasingly fascinated by frameworks like Scrum, they are mandating their IT departments to “go agile” and then, sooner or later…governance!</p>
</div>
<!-- Read More Link -->
<a class="summary-read-more-link" href="/agile-matters/2017/5/29/dont-let-governance-threaten-your-agile-transformation">Read More →</a>

<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--primary">
</div>
<div class="summary-metadata summary-metadata--secondary">
</div>
